> help
One action = one game-hour; queries take no time. Sleep keeps you in bed at least 4 hours (8 when you camp); wait to sleep on.
Eat defaults to ONE serving; use eat hardtack 2 for two. Need effects use the previous hour's activity.
Out on open ground with bedding, sleep makes camp where you stand; wait follows your lodging order, returning to a nearby paid or own bed when available.
look | signs | investigate <sign id> | actions [page] | go <place name/id> | talk <person> | <interaction> <person>
buy <item> [qty] | sell <item> [qty] | eat <item> [servings] [method] | cook <item> [servings] [method]
drink | meal | sleep | rest | work | wait <Nh|Nd> | wait until <HH:00|dawn|morning|noon|dusk|night|tomorrow>
prices | places [page] | standing <sleep|eat|drink|warmth|lodging> <on|off> | agency <commanded|psyche>
inspect <person/item/place> | pack <item> into <container> (moves one) | unpack <item>
aid <person> | retire | depart | rations <10..150> | plan | journal (lists what you owe) | explain <event id>
Enter a menu number or stable action id. help

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern. Rain in the evening.
1855-04-01 01:00 Sunday. Michael Dunleavy helped me, unasked. There are people in this county who keep the word neighbor alive.
1855-04-01 06:00 At Dunleavy's Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. I would like to know what made it.
1855-04-01 07:00 Michael Dunleavy helped Lucius Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-01 13:00 Magdalena Brenner helped Tollef Haugen at Iron Ford Lutheran Church. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-01 19:00 Michael Dunleavy has been good to Joel Pike, one kindness after another. Nobody asked him to. I think the better of the county for having him in it.
1855-04-01 20:00 It is the common report that Miss Ellis Wayfarer has been paying court to Mr. J. Pike at Dunleavy's Tavern.
1855-04-01 20:00 I paid court to Joel Pike at Dunleavy's Tavern on Sunday of an evening. My heart did a thing it has no business doing in a person of sense.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. The full moon. The bluffs stand out white and the dogs are restless.
1855-04-02 01:00 Joel Pike made eyes at me at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the small hours. I said something clever and have been trying to remember what.
Since last look: no time passed.

> help Hester Fenn
One action = one game-hour; queries take no time. Sleep keeps you in bed at least 4 hours (8 when you camp); wait to sleep on.
Eat defaults to ONE serving; use eat hardtack 2 for two. Need effects use the previous hour's activity.
Out on open ground with bedding, sleep makes camp where you stand; wait follows your lodging order, returning to a nearby paid or own bed when available.

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern. Rain in the evening.
1855-04-01 01:00 Sunday. Michael Dunleavy helped me, unasked. There are people in this county who keep the word neighbor alive.
1855-04-01 06:00 At Dunleavy's Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. I would like to know what made it.
1855-04-01 07:00 Michael Dunleavy helped Lucius Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-01 13:00 Magdalena Brenner helped Tollef Haugen at Iron Ford Lutheran Church. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-01 19:00 Michael Dunleavy has been good to Joel Pike, one kindness after another. Nobody asked him to. I think the better of the county for having him in it.
1855-04-01 20:00 It is the common report that Miss Ellis Wayfarer has been paying court to Mr. J. Pike at Dunleavy's Tavern.
1855-04-01 20:00 I paid court to Joel Pike at Dunleavy's Tavern on Sunday of an evening. My heart did a thing it has no business doing in a person of sense.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. The full moon. The bluffs stand out white and the dogs are restless.
1855-04-02 01:00 Joel Pike made eyes at me at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the small hours. I said something clever and have been trying to remember what.
1855-04-02 07:00 Hester Fenn comforted Minnie Fenn at Fenn Farm. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-02 11:00 Hester Fenn helped Minnie Fenn at Fenn Farm. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-02 13:00 I shut a door in myself today. I cannot say which, only that the house is quieter.
1855-04-02 17:00 Miss Ellis Wayfarer aimed a hard word at Mr. L. Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern and hit nothing; Mr. L. Vance was seen to smile, which is worse.
1855-04-02 17:00 I insulted Lucius Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the afternoon, and was sorry before the day was out.
1855-04-02 17:00 Amos Whitcomb told a tale to Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the afternoon.
1855-04-02 18:00 I made a gift to Joel Pike at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday of an evening. It cost me little and, I think, meant a good deal.
1855-04-02 18:00 Michael Dunleavy was in close talk with Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday of an evening. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 19:00 I asked the news of Amos Whitcomb at Dunleavy's Tavern. I came away knowing more than I went in with, which is the point of such talks.
1855-04-02 21:00 It was the sensible thing; anyone would agree. I have asked myself and I agree.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. Full moon, and bright enough to read a letter by.
1855-04-03 06:00 Nora Dunleavy was in close talk with Amos Whitcomb at Dunleavy's Tavern on Tuesday at first light. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-03 08:00 I notice I am saying the same thing to myself over and over, in the same words. A person ought to be able to say a thing once and be done.
1855-04-03 16:00 Michael Dunleavy was in close talk with Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Tuesday in the afternoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-03 18:00 Amos Whitcomb helped Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern. Rain in the evening.
1855-04-01 01:00 Sunday. Michael Dunleavy helped me, unasked. There are people in this county who keep the word neighbor alive.
1855-04-01 06:00 At Dunleavy's Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. I would like to know what made it.
1855-04-01 07:00 Michael Dunleavy helped Lucius Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-01 13:00 Magdalena Brenner helped Tollef Haugen at Iron Ford Lutheran Church. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-01 19:00 Michael Dunleavy has been good to Joel Pike, one kindness after another. Nobody asked him to. I think the better of the county for having him in it.
1855-04-01 20:00 It is the common report that Miss Ellis Wayfarer has been paying court to Mr. J. Pike at Dunleavy's Tavern.
1855-04-01 20:00 I paid court to Joel Pike at Dunleavy's Tavern on Sunday of an evening. My heart did a thing it has no business doing in a person of sense.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. The full moon. The bluffs stand out white and the dogs are restless.
1855-04-02 01:00 Joel Pike made eyes at me at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the small hours. I said something clever and have been trying to remember what.
1855-04-02 07:00 Hester Fenn comforted Minnie Fenn at Fenn Farm. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-02 11:00 Hester Fenn helped Minnie Fenn at Fenn Farm. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-02 13:00 I shut a door in myself today. I cannot say which, only that the house is quieter.
1855-04-02 17:00 Miss Ellis Wayfarer aimed a hard word at Mr. L. Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern and hit nothing; Mr. L. Vance was seen to smile, which is worse.
1855-04-02 17:00 I insulted Lucius Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the afternoon, and was sorry before the day was out.
1855-04-02 17:00 Amos Whitcomb told a tale to Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the afternoon.
1855-04-02 18:00 I made a gift to Joel Pike at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday of an evening. It cost me little and, I think, meant a good deal.
1855-04-02 18:00 Michael Dunleavy was in close talk with Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday of an evening. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 19:00 I asked the news of Amos Whitcomb at Dunleavy's Tavern. I came away knowing more than I went in with, which is the point of such talks.
1855-04-02 21:00 It was the sensible thing; anyone would agree. I have asked myself and I agree.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. Full moon, and bright enough to read a letter by.
1855-04-03 06:00 Nora Dunleavy was in close talk with Amos Whitcomb at Dunleavy's Tavern on Tuesday at first light. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-03 08:00 I notice I am saying the same thing to myself over and over, in the same words. A person ought to be able to say a thing once and be done.
1855-04-03 16:00 Michael Dunleavy was in close talk with Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Tuesday in the afternoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-03 18:00 Amos Whitcomb helped Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-04 00:00 Wednesday, the 4th of April, 1855. The waning gibbous. Snow in the morning.
1855-04-04 18:00 Nora Dunleavy owes me, and neither of us mentions it. I do not like being owed. It makes me watchful, and watchfulness is a poor friend.
Since last look: no time passed.

1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern. Rain in the evening.
1855-04-01 01:00 Sunday. Michael Dunleavy helped me, unasked. There are people in this county who keep the word neighbor alive.
1855-04-01 06:00 At Dunleavy's Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. I would like to know what made it.
1855-04-01 07:00 Michael Dunleavy helped Lucius Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-01 13:00 Magdalena Brenner helped Tollef Haugen at Iron Ford Lutheran Church. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-01 19:00 Michael Dunleavy has been good to Joel Pike, one kindness after another. Nobody asked him to. I think the better of the county for having him in it.
1855-04-01 20:00 It is the common report that Miss Ellis Wayfarer has been paying court to Mr. J. Pike at Dunleavy's Tavern.
1855-04-01 20:00 I paid court to Joel Pike at Dunleavy's Tavern on Sunday of an evening. My heart did a thing it has no business doing in a person of sense.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. The full moon. The bluffs stand out white and the dogs are restless.
1855-04-02 01:00 Joel Pike made eyes at me at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the small hours. I said something clever and have been trying to remember what.
1855-04-02 07:00 Hester Fenn comforted Minnie Fenn at Fenn Farm. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-02 11:00 Hester Fenn helped Minnie Fenn at Fenn Farm. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-02 13:00 I shut a door in myself today. I cannot say which, only that the house is quieter.
1855-04-02 17:00 Miss Ellis Wayfarer aimed a hard word at Mr. L. Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern and hit nothing; Mr. L. Vance was seen to smile, which is worse.
1855-04-02 17:00 I insulted Lucius Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the afternoon, and was sorry before the day was out.
1855-04-02 17:00 Amos Whitcomb told a tale to Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the afternoon.
1855-04-02 18:00 I made a gift to Joel Pike at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday of an evening. It cost me little and, I think, meant a good deal.
1855-04-02 18:00 Michael Dunleavy was in close talk with Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday of an evening. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 19:00 I asked the news of Amos Whitcomb at Dunleavy's Tavern. I came away knowing more than I went in with, which is the point of such talks.
1855-04-02 21:00 It was the sensible thing; anyone would agree. I have asked myself and I agree.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. Full moon, and bright enough to read a letter by.
1855-04-03 06:00 Nora Dunleavy was in close talk with Amos Whitcomb at Dunleavy's Tavern on Tuesday at first light. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-03 08:00 I notice I am saying the same thing to myself over and over, in the same words. A person ought to be able to say a thing once and be done.
1855-04-03 16:00 Michael Dunleavy was in close talk with Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Tuesday in the afternoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-03 18:00 Amos Whitcomb helped Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-04 00:00 Wednesday, the 4th of April, 1855. The waning gibbous. Snow in the morning.
1855-04-04 18:00 Nora Dunleavy owes me, and neither of us mentions it. I do not like being owed. It makes me watchful, and watchfulness is a poor friend.
Since last look: no time passed.

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern. Rain in the evening.
1855-04-01 01:00 Sunday. Michael Dunleavy helped me, unasked. There are people in this county who keep the word neighbor alive.
1855-04-01 06:00 At Dunleavy's Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. I would like to know what made it.
1855-04-01 07:00 Michael Dunleavy helped Lucius Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-01 13:00 Magdalena Brenner helped Tollef Haugen at Iron Ford Lutheran Church. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-01 19:00 Michael Dunleavy has been good to Joel Pike, one kindness after another. Nobody asked him to. I think the better of the county for having him in it.
1855-04-01 20:00 It is the common report that Miss Ellis Wayfarer has been paying court to Mr. J. Pike at Dunleavy's Tavern.
1855-04-01 20:00 I paid court to Joel Pike at Dunleavy's Tavern on Sunday of an evening. My heart did a thing it has no business doing in a person of sense.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. The full moon. The bluffs stand out white and the dogs are restless.
1855-04-02 01:00 Joel Pike made eyes at me at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the small hours. I said something clever and have been trying to remember what.
1855-04-02 07:00 Hester Fenn comforted Minnie Fenn at Fenn Farm. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-02 11:00 Hester Fenn helped Minnie Fenn at Fenn Farm. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-02 13:00 I shut a door in myself today. I cannot say which, only that the house is quieter.
1855-04-02 17:00 Miss Ellis Wayfarer aimed a hard word at Mr. L. Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern and hit nothing; Mr. L. Vance was seen to smile, which is worse.
1855-04-02 17:00 I insulted Lucius Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the afternoon, and was sorry before the day was out.
1855-04-02 17:00 Amos Whitcomb told a tale to Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the afternoon.
1855-04-02 18:00 I made a gift to Joel Pike at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday of an evening. It cost me little and, I think, meant a good deal.
1855-04-02 18:00 Michael Dunleavy was in close talk with Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday of an evening. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 19:00 I asked the news of Amos Whitcomb at Dunleavy's Tavern. I came away knowing more than I went in with, which is the point of such talks.
1855-04-02 21:00 It was the sensible thing; anyone would agree. I have asked myself and I agree.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. Full moon, and bright enough to read a letter by.
1855-04-03 06:00 Nora Dunleavy was in close talk with Amos Whitcomb at Dunleavy's Tavern on Tuesday at first light. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-03 08:00 I notice I am saying the same thing to myself over and over, in the same words. A person ought to be able to say a thing once and be done.
1855-04-03 16:00 Michael Dunleavy was in close talk with Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Tuesday in the afternoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-03 18:00 Amos Whitcomb helped Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-04 00:00 Wednesday, the 4th of April, 1855. The waning gibbous. Snow in the morning.
1855-04-04 18:00 Nora Dunleavy owes me, and neither of us mentions it. I do not like being owed. It makes me watchful, and watchfulness is a poor friend.
1855-04-04 19:00 Amos Whitcomb has not said a word of the money, which is worse than if he had.
1855-04-05 00:00 Thursday, the 5th of April, 1855, under the waning gibbous.
1855-04-05 00:00 I am down with want of food. Too long on salt meat and meal; the body keeps its own books.
1855-04-05 10:00 Nora Dunleavy helped Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-05 18:00 Minnie Fenn helped Edwin Fenn at Fenn Farm. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-05 18:00 Hester Fenn settled with Minnie Fenn at Fenn Farm on Thursday of an evening. One more account closed in a county full of open ones.
1855-04-05 19:00 I have a great many reasons this week, and I do not trust any of them. I would rather have one true thing.
1855-04-05 19:00 I was short with Minnie Fenn today and had no cause. Something else was the matter, and I will not name it.
1855-04-05 19:00 Johann Brenner insulted Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Thursday of an evening. I was by to see it.
1855-04-05 20:00 Nora Dunleavy comforted Johann Brenner at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-06 00:00 Friday, the 6th of April, 1855. The waning gibbous.
1855-04-06 08:00 Friday. Nora Dunleavy slighted me at Dunleavy's Tavern, and I shall not soon forget it.
1855-04-04 19:00 You owe Amos Whitcomb $1.50, due Friday, the 4th of May, 1855. Settling up is yours to order (repay Amos).
Since last look: no time passed.

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern. Rain in the evening.
1855-04-01 01:00 Sunday. Michael Dunleavy helped me, unasked. There are people in this county who keep the word neighbor alive.
1855-04-01 06:00 At Dunleavy's Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. I would like to know what made it.
1855-04-01 07:00 Michael Dunleavy helped Lucius Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-01 13:00 Magdalena Brenner helped Tollef Haugen at Iron Ford Lutheran Church. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-01 19:00 Michael Dunleavy has been good to Joel Pike, one kindness after another. Nobody asked him to. I think the better of the county for having him in it.
1855-04-01 20:00 It is the common report that Miss Ellis Wayfarer has been paying court to Mr. J. Pike at Dunleavy's Tavern.
1855-04-01 20:00 I paid court to Joel Pike at Dunleavy's Tavern on Sunday of an evening. My heart did a thing it has no business doing in a person of sense.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. The full moon. The bluffs stand out white and the dogs are restless.
1855-04-02 01:00 Joel Pike made eyes at me at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the small hours. I said something clever and have been trying to remember what.
1855-04-02 07:00 Hester Fenn comforted Minnie Fenn at Fenn Farm. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-02 11:00 Hester Fenn helped Minnie Fenn at Fenn Farm. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-02 13:00 I shut a door in myself today. I cannot say which, only that the house is quieter.
1855-04-02 17:00 Miss Ellis Wayfarer aimed a hard word at Mr. L. Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern and hit nothing; Mr. L. Vance was seen to smile, which is worse.
1855-04-02 17:00 I insulted Lucius Vance at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the afternoon, and was sorry before the day was out.
1855-04-02 17:00 Amos Whitcomb told a tale to Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday in the afternoon.
1855-04-02 18:00 I made a gift to Joel Pike at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday of an evening. It cost me little and, I think, meant a good deal.
1855-04-02 18:00 Michael Dunleavy was in close talk with Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Monday of an evening. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 19:00 I asked the news of Amos Whitcomb at Dunleavy's Tavern. I came away knowing more than I went in with, which is the point of such talks.
1855-04-02 21:00 It was the sensible thing; anyone would agree. I have asked myself and I agree.
1855-04-03 00:00 Tuesday, the 3rd of April, 1855. Full moon, and bright enough to read a letter by.
1855-04-03 06:00 Nora Dunleavy was in close talk with Amos Whitcomb at Dunleavy's Tavern on Tuesday at first light. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-03 08:00 I notice I am saying the same thing to myself over and over, in the same words. A person ought to be able to say a thing once and be done.
1855-04-03 16:00 Michael Dunleavy was in close talk with Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Tuesday in the afternoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-03 18:00 Amos Whitcomb helped Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-04 00:00 Wednesday, the 4th of April, 1855. The waning gibbous. Snow in the morning.
1855-04-04 18:00 Nora Dunleavy owes me, and neither of us mentions it. I do not like being owed. It makes me watchful, and watchfulness is a poor friend.
1855-04-04 19:00 Amos Whitcomb has not said a word of the money, which is worse than if he had.
1855-04-05 00:00 Thursday, the 5th of April, 1855, under the waning gibbous.
1855-04-05 00:00 I am down with want of food. Too long on salt meat and meal; the body keeps its own books.
1855-04-05 10:00 Nora Dunleavy helped Michael Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-05 18:00 Minnie Fenn helped Edwin Fenn at Fenn Farm. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-05 18:00 Hester Fenn settled with Minnie Fenn at Fenn Farm on Thursday of an evening. One more account closed in a county full of open ones.
1855-04-05 19:00 I have a great many reasons this week, and I do not trust any of them. I would rather have one true thing.
1855-04-05 19:00 I was short with Minnie Fenn today and had no cause. Something else was the matter, and I will not name it.
1855-04-05 19:00 Johann Brenner insulted Nora Dunleavy at Dunleavy's Tavern on Thursday of an evening. I was by to see it.
1855-04-05 20:00 Nora Dunleavy comforted Johann Brenner at Dunleavy's Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-06 00:00 Friday, the 6th of April, 1855. The waning gibbous.
1855-04-06 08:00 Friday. Nora Dunleavy slighted me at Dunleavy's Tavern, and I shall not soon forget it.
1855-04-06 11:00 I was short with Joel Pike today and had no cause. Something else was the matter, and I will not name it.
1855-04-06 17:00 I put this down before memory alters it. Huldra's Shelf: A cow-bell sounds upslope, though nobody grazes stock here.
1855-04-06 19:00 There is nothing in it. I looked at it from three sides and there is nothing in it. I shall not think of it again.
1855-04-07 00:00 Saturday, the 7th of April, 1855, under the waning gibbous. Rain in the night.
Since last look: no time passed.
1855-04-07 07:00 | FAIR | Dunleavy's Tavern
Ellis Wayfarer — sleeping. Purse $41.71; household $2.50. 36°F, dry.
Food 3.0 days; Medium, 25.9 kg.
Vitals: Hunger 727; Rest 1000; Thirst 736; Warmth 1000
Health: Vigor 7/7; Wounds 0/12. malnourished
Counter open; hours 6:00–23:00.
Board: bed $0.25 until noon; meal $0.20 (type meal).
Tavern, lodging, and the justice of the peace's court. Spring at Dunleavy's Tavern. The wind carries the smell of wet earth. Amos Whitcomb, Joel Pike, Michael Dunleavy are here. You remember: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. The question is still open; use signs.
Open sign: A report of strange signs at Huldra's Shelf. (investigate 17:3:dawn)

Heard: Ingrid Tveit is of a talkative disposition
